<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.

</PULL_REQUESTS>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

     5	`commentjson` (Comment JSON) is a Python package that helps you create JSON

    27	    ...     "location": "Delhi, India", // Person's location

    29	    ...     # Section contains info about

    33	    ...         "eyes_color": "black",
    34	    ...         "height": "6"
    35	    ...     }
    36	    ... }"""
    37	    >>>
    38	    >>> json_loaded = commentjson.loads(json_string)
    39	    >>> print json_loaded
    40	    {u'appearance': {u'eyes_color': u'black', u'hair_color': u'black', u'height': u'6'}, u'name': u'Vaidik Kapoor', u'location': u'Delhi, India'}

### 1.1-1.5 Problem Analysis

**Problem Statement**: 
The user wants to add support for trailing commas in JSON files. Currently, when commenting out the last item in a list or dictionary, the previous item retains its trailing comma, which makes the JSON invalid according to strict JSON parsing rules.

**Technical Details**:
- Method names: likely involves `loads()` function for parsing
- File names: `commentjson.py` is the main implementation file
- Error scenario: JSON with trailing commas after commenting out last items

**Clear Problem Description**:
The commentjson library currently follows strict JSON parsing rules that don't allow trailing commas. However, for a common workflow where developers comment/uncomment items in JSON files, trailing commas become problematic. When the last item is commented out, the new last item has a trailing comma that makes the JSON invalid.

**Steps to Reproduce**:
1. Create a JSON file with multiple items, each having trailing commas
2. Comment out the last item
3. Try to parse the resulting JSON - it should fail due to trailing comma on the new last item

**Best Practices for Testing/Fixing**:
- Minimal changes to existing functionality
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Follow existing code patterns in the repository
- Test both arrays and objects with trailing commas

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python
     2	# -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
     3	"""Add JavaScript or Python style comments in JSON.
     4	
     5	commentjson (Comment JSON) is a Python package that helps you create JSON files
     6	with Python and JavaScript style inline comments. Its API is very similar to
     7	the Python standard library’s json module.
     8	
     9	"""
    10	
    11	from __future__ import print_function
    12	from __future__ import division
    13	from __future__ import absolute_import
    14	from __future__ import unicode_literals
    15	
    16	import codecs
    17	import traceback
    18	
    19	try:
    20	    import json
    21	except ImportError:
    22	    # If python version is 2.5 or less, use simplejson
    23	    import simplejson as json
    24	
    25	
    26	import lark
    27	
    28	from lark import Lark
    29	from lark.reconstruct import Reconstructor
    30	
    31	
    32	parser = Lark('''
    33	    ?start: value
    34	    ?value: object
    35	          | array
    36	          | string
    37	          | SIGNED_NUMBER      -> number
    38	          | "true"             -> true
    39	          | "false"            -> false
    40	          | "null"             -> null
    41	    array  : "[" [value ("," value)*] "]"
    42	    object : "{" [pair ("," pair)*] "}"
    43	    pair   : string ":" value
    44	    string : ESCAPED_STRING
    45	
    46	    COMMENT: /(#|\\/\\/)[^\\n]*/
    47	
    48	    %import common.ESCAPED_STRING
    49	    %import common.SIGNED_NUMBER
    50	    %import common.WS
    51	    %ignore WS
    52	    %ignore COMMENT
    53	''', maybe_placeholders=False)
    54	
    55	serializer = Reconstructor(parser)
    56	
    57	
    58	def detect_encoding(b):
    59	    '''
    60	    Taken from `json` package in CPython 3.7.
    61	
    62	    Source can be found at https://bit.ly/2OHqCIK.
    63	    '''
    64	
    65	    bstartswith = b.startswith
    66	    if bstartswith((codecs.BOM_UTF32_BE, codecs.BOM_UTF32_LE)):
    67	        return 'utf-32'
    68	    if bstartswith((codecs.BOM_UTF16_BE, codecs.BOM_UTF16_LE)):
    69	        return 'utf-16'
    70	    if bstartswith(codecs.BOM_UTF8):
    71	        return 'utf-8-sig'
    72	
    73	    if len(b) >= 4:
    74	        if not b[0]:
    75	            # 00 00 -- -- - utf-32-be
    76	            # 00 XX -- -- - utf-16-be
    77	            return 'utf-16-be' if b[1] else 'utf-32-be'
    78	        if not b[1]:
    79	            # XX 00 00 00 - utf-32-le
    80	            # XX 00 00 XX - utf-16-le
    81	            # XX 00 XX -- - utf-16-le
    82	            return 'utf-16-le' if b[2] or b[3] else 'utf-32-le'
    83	    elif len(b) == 2:
    84	        if not b[0]:
    85	            # 00 XX - utf-16-be
    86	            return 'utf-16-be'
    87	        if not b[1]:
    88	            # XX 00 - utf-16-le
    89	            return 'utf-16-le'
    90	    # default
    91	    return 'utf-8'
    92	
    93	
    94	class BaseException(Exception):
    95	    ''' Base exception to be implemented and raised while handling exceptions
    96	    raised by libraries used in `commentjson`.
    97	
    98	    Sets message of self in a way that it clearly calls out that the exception
    99	    was raised by another library, along with the entire stacktrace of the
   100	    exception raised by the other library.
   101	    '''
   102	
   103	    def __init__(self, exc):
   104	        if self.library is None:
   105	            raise NotImplementedError(
   106	                'Value of library must be set in the '
   107	                'inherited exception class.')
   108	
   109	        tb = traceback.format_exc()
   110	        tb = '\n'.join(' ' * 4 + line_ for line_ in tb.split('\n'))
   111	
   112	        error = None
   113	        try:
   114	            error = exc.msg
   115	        except AttributeError:
   116	            try:
   117	                error = exc.message
   118	            except AttributeError:
   119	                error = str(exc)
   120	
   121	        self.message = '\n'.join([
   122	            'JSON Library Exception\n',
   123	            ('Exception thrown by library (%s): '
   124	             '\033[4;37m%s\033[0m\n' % (self.library, error)),
   125	            '%s' % tb,
   126	        ])
   127	        Exception.__init__(self, self.message)
   128	
   129	
   130	class ParserException(BaseException):
   131	    '''Exception raised when the `lark` raises an exception i.e.
   132	    the exception is not caused by `commentjson` and caused by the use of
   133	    `lark` in `commentjson`.
   134	    '''
   135	
   136	    library = 'lark'
   137	
   138	
   139	class JSONLibraryException(BaseException):
   140	    '''Exception raised when the `json` raises an exception i.e.
   141	    the exception is not caused by `commentjson` and caused by the use of
   142	    `json` in `commentjson`.
   143	
   144	    .. note::
   145	
   146	        As of now, ``commentjson`` supports only standard library's ``json``
   147	        module. It might start supporting other widely-used contributed JSON
   148	        libraries in the future.
   149	    '''
   150	
   151	    library = 'json'
   152	
   153	
   154	def loads(text, *args, **kwargs):
   155	    ''' Deserialize `text` (a `str` or `unicode` instance containing a JSON
   156	    document with Python or JavaScript like comments) to a Python object.
   157	
   158	    :param text: serialized JSON string with or without comments.
   159	    :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.loads <http://docs.python.org/
   160	                   2/library/json.html#json.loads>`_ accepts.
   161	    :returns: dict or list.
   162	    '''
   163	
   164	    if isinstance(text, (bytes, bytearray)):
   165	        text = text.decode(detect_encoding(text), 'surrogatepass')
   166	
   167	    try:
   168	        parsed = parser.parse(text)
   169	        final_text = serializer.reconstruct(parsed)
   170	    except lark.exceptions.UnexpectedCharacters:
   171	        raise ValueError('Unable to parse text', text)
   172	
   173	    return json.loads(final_text, *args, **kwargs)
   174	
   175	
   176	def dumps(obj, **kwargs):
   177	    ''' Serialize `obj` to a JSON formatted `str`. Accepts the same arguments
   178	    as `json` module in stdlib.
   179	
   180	    :param obj: a JSON serializable Python object.
   181	    :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.dumps <http://docs.python.org/
   182	                   2/library/json.html#json.dumps>`_ accepts.
   183	    :raises: commentjson.JSONLibraryException
   184	    :returns str: serialized string.
   185	    '''
   186	
   187	    return json.dumps(obj, **kwargs)
   188	
   189	
   190	def load(fp, **kwargs):
   191	    ''' Deserialize `fp` (a `.read()`-supporting file-like object containing a
   192	    JSON document with Python or JavaScript like comments) to a Python object.
   193	
   194	    :param fp: a `.read()`-supporting file-like object containing a JSON
   195	               document with or without comments.
   196	    :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.load <http://docs.python.org/
   197	                   2/library/json.html#json.load>`_ accepts.
   198	    :raises: commentjson.JSONLibraryException
   199	    :returns: dict or list.
   200	    '''
   201	
   202	    try:
   203	        return loads(fp.read(), **kwargs)
   204	    except Exception as e:
   205	        raise JSONLibraryException(e)
   206	
   207	
   208	def dump(obj, fp, **kwargs):
   209	    ''' Serialize `obj` as a JSON formatted stream to `fp` (a
   210	    `.write()`-supporting file-like object). Accepts the same arguments as
   211	    `json` module in stdlib.
   212	
   213	    :param obj: a JSON serializable Python object.
   214	    :param fp: a `.read()`-supporting file-like object containing a JSON
   215	               document with or without comments.
   216	    :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.dump <http://docs.python.org/
   217	                   2/library/json.html#json.dump>`_ accepts.
   218	    :raises: commentjson.JSONLibraryException
   219	    '''
   220	
   221	    try:
   222	        json.dump(obj, fp, **kwargs)
   223	    except Exception as e:
   224	        raise JSONLibraryException(e)
   225

    13	class TestCommentJson(unittest.TestCase):
    14	
    15	    def setUp(self):
    16	        self.test_json = {}
    17	        self.path = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
    18	        self.files = ('sample', 'line_comment', 'inline_last_float',
    19	                      'inline_last_int', 'nested_object', 'string_with_hash',

    30	            with open('%s-commented.json' % fpath) as fp:
    31	                commented = fp.read()
    32	
    33	            self.test_json.update({
    34	                file_: {
    35	                    'uncommented': uncommented,
    36	                    'commented': commented,
    37	                },
    38	            })
    39	
    40	    def tearDown(self):
    41	        test_file_path = os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json')
    42	        if os.path.exists(test_file_path):
    43	            os.unlink(test_file_path)

    45	    def test_dumping_parsing_simple_string(self):
    46	        string = '//'
    47	        self.assertEqual(commentjson.loads(commentjson.dumps(string)), string)
    48	
    49	        string = '#'
    50	        self.assertEqual(commentjson.loads(commentjson.dumps(string)), string)
    51	
    52	    def test_dumps(self):
    53	        test_dict = dict(a=1, b=2)
    54	        c_dump = commentjson.dumps(test_dict)
    55	        j_dump = json.dumps(test_dict)
    56	        assert c_dump, j_dump
    57	
    58	    def test_dumps_with_kwargs(self):
    59	        test_dict = dict(a=1, b=2)
    60	        test_kwargs = dict(indent=4)
    61	
    62	        c_dump = commentjson.dumps(test_dict, **test_kwargs)
    63	        j_dump = json.dumps(test_dict, **test_kwargs)
    64	        assert c_dump, j_dump
    65	
    66	    def test_dumps_throws_exception(self):
    67	        class Unserializable:
    68	            pass
    69	        self.assertRaises(TypeError, commentjson.dumps,
    70	                          Unserializable)
    71	
    72	    def test_loads(self):
    73	        for index, test_json_ in iteritems(self.test_json):
    74	            commented = test_json_['commented']
    75	            uncommented = test_json_['uncommented']
    76	            self.assertEqual(
    77	                commentjson.loads(commented),
    78	                json.loads(uncommented),
    79	                'Failed for test: %s' % test_json_['commented'])
    80	
    81	    def test_loads_with_kwargs(self):
    82	        def test_hook(loaded_dict):
    83	            return {}
    84	        commented = self.test_json['sample']['commented']
    85	        test_kwargs = dict(object_hook=test_hook)
    86	
    87	        c_load = commentjson.loads(commented, **test_kwargs)
    88	
    89	        # make sure that object_hook did its work
    90	        assert c_load == {}
    91	
    92	    def test_loads_throws_exception(self):
    93	        self.assertRaises(ValueError, commentjson.loads,
    94	                          'Unserializable text')
    95	
    96	    def test_dump(self):
    97	        test_dict = dict(a=1, b=2)
    98	
    99	        wfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json'), 'w')
   100	        commentjson.dump(test_dict, wfp)
   101	        wfp.close()
   102	
   103	        rfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json'), 'r')
   104	        j_dump = json.dumps(test_dict)
   105	
   106	        assert rfp.read(), j_dump
   107	        rfp.close()
   108	
   109	    def test_dump_with_kwargs(self):
   110	        test_dict = dict(a=1, b=2)
   111	        test_kwargs = dict(indent=4)
   112	
   113	        wfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json'), 'w')
   114	        commentjson.dump(test_dict, wfp, **test_kwargs)
   115	        wfp.close()
   116	
   117	        rfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json'), 'r')
   118	        j_dump = json.dumps(test_dict, **test_kwargs)
   119	        c_dump = rfp.read()
   120	
   121	        assert c_dump == j_dump, c_dump
   122	        rfp.close()
   123	
   124	    def test_dump_throws_exception(self):
   125	        class Unserializable:
   126	            pass
   127	        fp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'test.json'), 'w')
   128	        self.assertRaises(commentjson.JSONLibraryException, commentjson.dump,
   129	                          Unserializable, fp)

   131	
   132	    def test_load(self):

   134	            rfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, '%s-commented.json' % file_),
   135	                       'r')
   136	            uncommented = self.test_json[file_]['uncommented']
   137	            assert commentjson.load(rfp) == json.loads(uncommented)
   138	            rfp.close()
   139

   141	        def test_hook(loaded_dict):
   142	            return {}
   143

   145	        rfp = open(os.path.join(self.path, 'sample-commented.json'), 'r')
   146	
   147	        self.assertEqual(commentjson.load(rfp, **test_kwargs), {})
   148	        rfp.close()
   149	
   150	    def test_load_throws_exception(self):

```
array  : "[" [value ("," value)*] "]"
object : "{" [pair ("," pair)*] "}"
```

To support trailing commas, I need to modify these to allow an optional trailing comma. The syntax should be:

```
array  : "[" [value ("," value)* ","?] "]"
object : "{" [pair ("," pair)* ","?] "}"
```

Or alternatively:

```
array  : "[" [value ("," value)* [","]] "]"
object : "{" [pair ("," pair)* [","]] "}"
```

The second approach with square brackets is cleaner and more explicit about the optional nature of the trailing comma.

Let me also check if there are any other places where this needs to be handled. Since the parser strips comments and then reconstructs the JSON, and then passes it to the standard json library, the reconstructed JSON should not have trailing commas for it to work with the standard library.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The commentjson library currently uses a strict JSON grammar that doesn't allow trailing commas in arrays or objects. This makes it difficult for users to comment/uncomment items because when the last item is commented out, the previous item retains a trailing comma which makes the JSON invalid.

### 5.2 Problem Location
The problem is located in `/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py` at lines 41-42 in the Lark grammar definition:
```lark
array  : "[" [value ("," value)*] "]"
object : "{" [pair ("," pair)*] "}"
```

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue
The test script creates JSON strings with trailing commas and attempts to parse them using `commentjson.loads()`. All cases fail with `ValueError: Unable to parse text` because the Lark parser rejects the trailing commas.

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix
- Make minimal changes to the existing grammar
- Maintain backward compatibility (existing valid JSON should still work)
- Follow Lark grammar syntax conventions
- Ensure both arrays and objects support trailing commas
- Don't break existing comment handling functionality

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem
Modify the Lark grammar rules to allow optional trailing commas:
```lark
array  : "[" [value ("," value)* [","]] "]"
object : "{" [pair ("," pair)* [","]] "}"
```

The `[","]` part makes the trailing comma optional, allowing both formats to work.

from lark import Lark
from lark.reconstruct import Reconstructor

# Try a cleaner grammar without ambiguity
try:
    parser = Lark('''
        ?start: value
        ?value: object
              | array
              | string
              | SIGNED_NUMBER      -> number
              | \"true\"             -> true
              | \"false\"            -> false
              | \"null\"             -> null
        array  : \"[\" \"]\"
               | \"[\" nonempty_array_content \"]\"
        nonempty_array_content : value (\",\" value)* \",\"?
        object : \"{\" \"}\"
               | \"{\" nonempty_object_content \"}\"
        nonempty_object_content : pair (\",\" pair)* \",\"?
        pair   : string \":\" value
        string : ESCAPED_STRING

        %import common.ESCAPED_STRING
        %import common.SIGNED_NUMBER
        %import common.WS
        %ignore WS
    ''')
    print('Parser created successfully')
    reconstructor = Reconstructor(parser)
    print('Reconstructor created successfully')
    
    # Test parsing with trailing comma
    text = '[\"a\", \"b\",]'
    parsed = parser.parse(text)
    print('Parsed successfully:', parsed.pretty())
    
    reconstructed = reconstructor.reconstruct(parsed)
    print('Reconstructed:', repr(reconstructed))

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! I can modify the `loads` function to preprocess the text. Let me add a function to remove trailing commas:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    import re
    
    # Pattern to match trailing commas in arrays: ,] -> ]
    # But we need to be careful about strings and comments
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            while j < len(result) and stack:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle string literals
                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            stack.pop()
                            if not stack:  # Found matching closing bracket
                                # Now look for trailing comma in this section
                                section = result[i:j+1]
                                # Simple regex approach for this section
                                # Replace ", ]" with "]" and ", }" with "}" 
                                # but be careful about positions
                                new_section = section
                                # Work backwards from the end to avoid index issues
                                k = len(new_section) - 2  # Start before the closing bracket
                                while k >= 0:
                                    if new_section[k] == ',':
                                        # Check if this comma is followed only by whitespace until the closing bracket
                                        l = k + 1
                                        while l < len(new_section) and new_section[l] in ' \t\r\n':
                                            l += 1
                                        if l < len(new_section) and new_section[l] in ']}':
                                            # This is a trailing comma, remove it
                                            new_section = new_section[:k] + new_section[k+1:]
                                    k -= 1
                                
                                if new_section != section:
                                    result = result[:i] + new_section + result[j+1:]
                                    # Adjust indices since we modified the string
                                    diff = len(new_section) - len(section)
                                    j += diff
                                break
                    elif char == ',':
                        # Just continue, comma is fine
                        pass
                
                j += 1
            
            i = j if j > i else i + 1
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result

"""Debug the trailing comma removal function."""

import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8')

from commentjson.commentjson import remove_trailing_commas

def test_remove_trailing_commas():
    """Test the trailing comma removal function."""
    
    # Simple test cases
    test_cases = [
        # Basic array with trailing comma
        ('[1, 2,]', '[1, 2]'),
        
        # Basic object with trailing comma  
        ('{"a": 1, "b": 2,}', '{"a": 1, "b": 2}'),
        
        # Nested structures
        ('{"arr": [1, 2,], "obj": {"x": 1,}}', '{"arr": [1, 2], "obj": {"x": 1}}'),
        
        # With comments (should preserve comments but remove trailing commas)
        ('[\n  1,\n  2,  // comment\n]', '[\n  1,\n  2  // comment\n]'),
    ]
    
    for i, (input_text, expected) in enumerate(test_cases):
        print(f"\nTest case {i+1}:")

Let me improve the `remove_trailing_commas` function:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            while j < len(result) and stack:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle string literals
                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            stack.pop()
                            if not stack:  # Found matching closing bracket
                                # Now look for trailing comma in this section
                                # We need to work backwards from the closing bracket
                                # to find trailing commas followed by whitespace/comments
                                section_end = j
                                k = j - 1
                                
                                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                                while k > i:
                                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                                        k -= 1
                                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                                        # Found end of // comment, skip to start of line or previous non-whitespace
                                        k -= 2
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i and result[k] in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                    elif result[k] == '#':
                                        # Found start of # comment, skip to start of line or previous non-whitespace
                                        k -= 1
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i and result[k] in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                    else:
                                        break
                                
                                # Now check if the character before whitespace/comments is a comma
                                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                                    # This is a trailing comma, remove it
                                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                                    # Adjust indices since we modified the string
                                    j -= 1
                                break
                    elif char == ',':
                        # Just continue, comma is fine
                        pass
                
                j += 1
            
            i = j if j > i else i + 1
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    import re
    
    # Pattern to match trailing commas in arrays: ,] -> ]
    # But we need to be careful about strings and comments
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            while j < len(result) and stack:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle string literals
                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            stack.pop()
                            if not stack:  # Found matching closing bracket
                                # Now look for trailing comma in this section
                                section = result[i:j+1]
                                # Simple regex approach for this section
                                # Replace ", ]" with "]" and ", }" with "}" 
                                # but be careful about positions
                                new_section = section
                                # Work backwards from the end to avoid index issues
                                k = len(new_section) - 2  # Start before the closing bracket
                                while k >= 0:
                                    if new_section[k] == ',':
                                        # Check if this comma is followed only by whitespace until the closing bracket
                                        l = k + 1
                                        while l < len(new_section) and new_section[l] in ' \t\r\n':
                                            l += 1
                                        if l < len(new_section) and new_section[l] in ']}':
                                            # This is a trailing comma, remove it
                                            new_section = new_section[:k] + new_section[k+1:]
                                    k -= 1
                                
                                if new_section != section:
                                    result = result[:i] + new_section + result[j+1:]
                                    # Adjust indices since we modified the string
                                    diff = len(new_section) - len(section)
                                    j += diff
                                break
                    elif char == ',':
                        # Just continue, comma is fine
                        pass
                
                j += 1
            
            i = j if j > i else i + 1
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited. """Detailed debug of the comma removal logic."""

def debug_remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Debug version with print statements.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    iteration = 0
    while i < len(result):
        iteration += 1
        if iteration > 100:  # Prevent infinite loop
            print("Breaking due to iteration limit")
            break
            
        print(f"Iteration {iteration}, i={i}, char='{result[i] if i < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            print(f"Found opening bracket at position {i}: '{result[i]}'")
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            print(f"Starting scan from position {j}")
            while j < len(result) and stack:
                char = result[j]
                print(f"  Position {j}: char='{char}', in_string={in_string}, in_comment={in_comment}")
                
                # Handle string literals
                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                        print("    Entering string")
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                        print("    Exiting string")
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                    print("    Escape next character")
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                    print("    End escape")
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                    print("    Entering # comment")
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                    print("    Entering // comment")
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                    print("    Exiting # comment")
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                    print("    Exiting // comment")
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                        print(f"    Pushing to stack: {stack}")
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            popped = stack.pop()
                            print(f"    Popping from stack: {popped}, remaining: {stack}")
                            if not stack:  # Found matching closing bracket
                                print(f"    Found matching closing bracket at position {j}")
                                # Now look for trailing comma in this section
                                # We need to work backwards from the closing bracket
                                # to find trailing commas followed by whitespace/comments
                                print("    Scanning backwards for trailing comma...")
                                k = j - 1
                                
                                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                                while k > i:
                                    print(f"      Backwards scan at {k}: '{result[k]}'")
                                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                                        print(f"        Skipping whitespace")
                                        k -= 1
                                    elif k > 1 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                                        print(f"        Found // comment end, scanning backwards to line start")
                                        # Found end of // comment, skip to start of line or previous non-whitespace
                                        k -= 2
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i and result[k] in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                    elif result[k] == '#':
                                        print(f"        Found # comment start, scanning backwards to line start")
                                        # Found start of # comment, skip to start of line or previous non-whitespace
                                        k -= 1
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i and result[k] in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                    else:
                                        print(f"        Stopping at non-whitespace/comment: '{result[k]}'")
                                        break
                                
                                # Now check if the character before whitespace/comments is a comma
                                print(f"      Final position after backwards scan: {k}")
                                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                                    print(f"      Found trailing comma at position {k}!")
                                    # This is a trailing comma, remove it
                                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                                    print(f"      Removed comma, new length: {len(result)}")
                                    # Adjust indices since we modified the string
                                    j -= 1
                                else:
                                    print(f"      No trailing comma found")
                                break
                    elif char == ',':
                        print(f"    Found comma at position {j}")
                        # Just continue, comma is fine
                        pass
                
                j += 1
            
            i = j if j > i else i + 1
            print(f"Moving to next position: {i}")
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result

def main():
    failing_json = """{
        "feature1": "enabled",
        "feature2": "disabled",  // This comma becomes problematic
        // "feature3": "testing"
    }"""
    
    print("Processing:")
    print(repr(failing_json))
    print()
    
    result = debug_remove_trailing_commas(failing_json)
    print()
    print("Result:")
    print(repr(result))

    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]

            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            while j < len(result) and stack:

                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            stack.pop()

                                        if comment_start >= i:
                                            # Check if there's a // before this position
                                            line_start = comment_start + 1 if result[comment_start] in '\n\r' else comment_start
                                            if line_start + 1 < len(result) and result[line_start:line_start+2] == '//':
                                                # This is indeed a // comment, jump to line start
                                                k = comment_start - 1 if comment_start > i else i
                                            else:
                                                k -= 1
                                        else:
                                            k -= 1
                                    elif result[k] == '#':
                                        # Found start of # comment, skip to start of line
                                        k -= 1
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':

                                        if k > i:

        else:
            i += 1

Review the changes and make sure they are as expected. Edit the file again if necessary.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>This is getting overly complicated. Let me step back and think of a simpler approach. The issue is that I'm trying to be too smart about parsing comments when scanning backwards.

Instead, let me use a much simpler approach: I know that when I find a closing brace/bracket, everything between the opening and closing should be processed. I can extract that substring and process it more simply.

But even simpler - let me just write a regex-based solution that's much cleaner:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    import re
    
    # Pattern to match trailing commas in arrays and objects
    # This regex looks for a comma followed by optional whitespace and comments,
    # ending with a closing bracket or brace
    def replace_trailing_comma(match):
        # Return everything except the trailing comma
        prefix = match.group(1)  # Everything before the comma
        suffix = match.group(2)  # Whitespace and comments after comma
        closing = match.group(3)  # Closing bracket/brace
        return prefix + suffix + closing
    
    # Handle both arrays [...] and objects {...}
    # Match: (content before comma)(comma)(whitespace and comments)(closing bracket)
    pattern = r'([^,\\]*)((?:,(?=//)|,(?=#)|,)[\s\S]*?)([]}])(?=\s*(?:,[\]\}])|$|[\]\}])'
    
    # Simpler approach: process character by character but with better logic
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, find matching closing one
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                while k > i:
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    elif result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards  
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    else:
                        break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] == '[' or result[i] == '{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, look for matching closing one
            stack = [result[i]]
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_comment = False
            comment_type = None  # '#' or '//'
            
            while j < len(result) and stack:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle string literals
                if not in_comment and char == '"' and not escape_next:
                    if not in_string:
                        in_string = True
                    else:
                        in_string = False
                elif not in_comment and char == '\\' and in_string:
                    escape_next = not escape_next
                elif not in_comment and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ['\n', '\r', ' ', '\t']):
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '#'
                elif not in_string and not in_comment and char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j+1] == '/':
                    in_comment = True
                    comment_type = '//'
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '#' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                elif in_comment and comment_type == '//' and char in ['\n', '\r']:
                    in_comment = False
                    comment_type = None
                
                # Handle brackets and commas when not in string or comment
                elif not in_string and not in_comment:
                    if char == '[' or char == '{':
                        stack.append(char)
                    elif char == ']' or char == '}':
                        if stack:
                            stack.pop()
                            if not stack:  # Found matching closing bracket
                                # Now look for trailing comma in this section
                                # We need to work backwards from the closing bracket
                                # to find trailing commas followed by whitespace/comments
                                k = j - 1
                                
                                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                                # We need to be smarter about this - track comment boundaries
                                while k > i:
                                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                                        k -= 1
                                    elif k > 1 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                                        # Found end of // comment, skip to start of line
                                        k -= 2
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i:
                                            k -= 1  # Skip the newline
                                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '\n' and result[k-1] == '/':
                                        # This might be confusing our logic, let's be more careful
                                        # Actually, we should check if this is the start of a // comment
                                        # Look for // pattern
                                        comment_start = k - 1
                                        while comment_start > i and result[comment_start] not in '\n\r':
                                            comment_start -= 1
                                        if comment_start >= i:
                                            # Check if there's a // before this position
                                            line_start = comment_start + 1 if result[comment_start] in '\n\r' else comment_start
                                            if line_start + 1 < len(result) and result[line_start:line_start+2] == '//':
                                                # This is indeed a // comment, jump to line start
                                                k = comment_start - 1 if comment_start > i else i
                                            else:
                                                k -= 1
                                        else:
                                            k -= 1
                                    elif result[k] == '#':
                                        # Found start of # comment, skip to start of line
                                        k -= 1
                                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                                            k -= 1
                                        if k > i:
                                            k -= 1  # Skip the newline
                                    else:
                                        break
                                
                                # Now check if the character before whitespace/comments is a comma
                                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                                    # This is a trailing comma, remove it
                                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                                    # Adjust indices since we modified the string
                                    j -= 1
                                break
                    elif char == ',':
                        # Just continue, comma is fine
                        pass
                
                j += 1
            
            i = j if j > i else i + 1
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited. """Simple debug to trace the comma removal."""

import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8')

# Copy the function here for debugging
def debug_remove_trailing_commas(text):
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        print(f"At position {i}: '{result[i] if i < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            print(f"Found opening {bracket_type} at position {i}")
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                old_in_string = in_string
                old_in_sl_comment = in_single_line_comment
                old_in_hash_comment = in_hash_comment
                old_level = level
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                if (old_in_string != in_string or old_in_sl_comment != in_single_line_comment or 
                    old_in_hash_comment != in_hash_comment or old_level != level):
                    print(f"  Position {j}: '{char}' -> string:{in_string}, //:{in_single_line_comment}, #:{in_hash_comment}, level:{level}")
                elif j % 20 == 0:  # Print every 20th character to avoid too much output
                    print(f"  Position {j}: '{char}' (skipping verbose output)")
                    
                j += 1
            
            print(f"Found closing {closing_bracket} at position {j-1 if level == 0 else 'not found'}")
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                print(f"Scanning backwards from {j-2} to {i}")
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                while k > i:
                    print(f"  Backwards scan at {k}: '{result[k]}'")
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        print(f"    Skipping whitespace")
                        k -= 1
                    elif k > 1 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        print(f"    Found // comment end, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip // comment backwards
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                            print(f"    Jumped to position {k}")
                    elif result[k] == '#':
                        print(f"    Found # comment start, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip # comment backwards  
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                            print(f"    Jumped to position {k}")
                    else:
                        print(f"    Stopped at '{result[k]}'")
                        break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                print(f"Final backwards position: {k}, char: '{result[k] if k < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    print(f"FOUND TRAILING COMMA AT POSITION {k} - REMOVING IT")
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    print(f"New result length: {len(result)}")
                else:
                    print("No trailing comma found")
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result

def main():
    failing_json = """{
        "feature1": "enabled",
        "feature2": "disabled",  // This comma becomes problematic
        // "feature3": "testing"
    }"""

Let me fix this by implementing a proper backwards comment-aware scanner:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                # We need to be comment-aware when scanning backwards too
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                while k > i:
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                    # Check if we're at the end of a // comment
                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    # Check if we're at a # comment
                    elif result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    else:
                        break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    import re
    
    # Pattern to match trailing commas in arrays and objects
    # This regex looks for a comma followed by optional whitespace and comments,
    # ending with a closing bracket or brace
    def replace_trailing_comma(match):
        # Return everything except the trailing comma
        prefix = match.group(1)  # Everything before the comma
        suffix = match.group(2)  # Whitespace and comments after comma
        closing = match.group(3)  # Closing bracket/brace
        return prefix + suffix + closing
    
    # Handle both arrays [...] and objects {...}
    # Match: (content before comma)(comma)(whitespace and comments)(closing bracket)
    pattern = r'([^,\\]*)((?:,(?=//)|,(?=#)|,)[\s\S]*?)([]}])(?=\s*(?:,[\]\}])|$|[\]\}])'
    
    # Simpler approach: process character by character but with better logic
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            # Found opening bracket/brace, find matching closing one
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                while k > i:
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    elif result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards  
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    else:
                        break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited.                             elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':

                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':

                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False

                    break

                    # Adjust j since we removed a character

    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
