            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                # We need to be comment-aware when scanning backwards too
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Skip whitespace and comments backwards
                while k > i:
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                    # Check if we're at the end of a // comment
                    elif k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    # Check if we're at a # comment
                    elif result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                    else:
                        break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited. Let me add some debug prints to the actual function to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                # We need to scan backwards while being aware of comments
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # DEBUG
                print(f"DEBUG: Found matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                print(f"DEBUG: Scanning backwards from {k} to {i}")
                
                # Scan backwards with full comment awareness
                while k > i:
                    # First, determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    in_comment_at_k = False
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is inside comment/string, skipping")
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is whitespace '{result[k]}', skipping")
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is end of // comment, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is # comment, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we reach here, we're at a significant character
                    # DEBUG
                    print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} has significant character '{result[k]}'")
                    break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                print(f"DEBUG: Final position {k}, character '{result[k] if k < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    print(f"DEBUG: FOUND TRAILING COMMA AT POSITION {k} - REMOVING")
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
                else:
                    print(f"DEBUG: No trailing comma found at position {k}")
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.

    while i < len(result):

            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'

            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                # We need to scan backwards while being aware of comments
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Scan backwards with full comment awareness
                while k > i:
                    # First, determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    in_comment_at_k = False
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we reach here, we're at a significant character
                    break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited.             closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'

                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':

                        in_hash_comment = False

                        if char == bracket_type:

                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket

                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False

                        k -= 1

                        continue

                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':

                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue

                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        break  # Done with this structure
                    
                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
                    break
                
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1

    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                # We need to scan backwards while being aware of comments
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # DEBUG
                print(f"DEBUG: Found matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                print(f"DEBUG: Scanning backwards from {k} to {i}")
                
                # Scan backwards with full comment awareness
                while k > i:
                    # First, determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    in_comment_at_k = False
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is inside comment/string, skipping")
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is whitespace '{result[k]}', skipping")
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is end of // comment, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # DEBUG
                        print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} is # comment, skipping backwards")
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we reach here, we're at a significant character
                    # DEBUG
                    print(f"DEBUG: Position {k} has significant character '{result[k]}'")
                    break
                
                # Check if we found a trailing comma
                print(f"DEBUG: Final position {k}, character '{result[k] if k < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
                if k > i and result[k] == ',':
                    print(f"DEBUG: FOUND TRAILING COMMA AT POSITION {k} - REMOVING")
                    # Remove the comma
                    result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                    # Adjust j since we removed a character
                    j -= 1
                else:
                    print(f"DEBUG: No trailing comma found at position {k}")
            
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited.     '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    print(f"DEBUG remove_trailing_commas called with text length {len(text)}")
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                print(f"DEBUG: Found matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Scan backwards, skipping whitespace and comments, looking for a comma
                while k > i:
                    # Determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment (the / character after another /)
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Skipping // comment at position {k}")
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment (the # character itself)
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Skipping # comment at position {k}")
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Found trailing comma at position {k}, removing")
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        break  # Done with this structure
                    
                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found significant character '{result[k]}' at position {k}, no trailing comma")
                    break
                
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    if len(result) != len(text):
        print(f"DEBUG: Removed {len(text) - len(result)} characters")
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):

   268	                        k -= 2
   269	                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
   270	                            k -= 1
   271	                        if k > i:
   272	                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
   273	                        continue
   274	                    
   275	                    # If we're at a # comment (the # character itself)
   276	                    if result[k] == '#':
   277	                        print(f"DEBUG: Skipping # comment at position {k}")
   278	                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
   279	                        k -= 1
   280	                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
   281	                            k -= 1
   282	                        if k > i:
   283	                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
   284	                        continue
   285	                    
   286	                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
   287	                    if result[k] == ',':
   288	                        print(f"DEBUG: Found trailing comma at position {k}, removing")
   289	                        # Remove the comma
   290	                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
   291	                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
   292	                        j -= 1
   293	                        break  # Done with this structure
   294	                    
   295	                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
   296	                    print(f"DEBUG: Found significant character '{result[k]}' at position {k}, no trailing comma")
   297	                    # Continue scanning backwards for potential comma
   298	                    k -= 1
   299	                
   300	            i = j
   301	        else:
   302	            i += 1

Let me clean up the debug prints and test the original issue scenarios:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Scan backwards, skipping whitespace and comments, looking for a comma
                while k > i:
                    # Determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment (the / character after another /)
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment (the # character itself)
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        break  # Done with this structure
                    
                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
                    # Continue scanning backwards for potential comma
                    k -= 1
                
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    print(f"DEBUG remove_trailing_commas called with text length {len(text)}")
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                print(f"DEBUG: Found matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Scan backwards, skipping whitespace and comments, looking for a comma
                while k > i:
                    # Determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment (the / character after another /)
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Skipping // comment at position {k}")
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment (the # character itself)
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Skipping # comment at position {k}")
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        print(f"DEBUG: Found trailing comma at position {k}, removing")
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        break  # Done with this structure
                    
                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
                    print(f"DEBUG: Found significant character '{result[k]}' at position {k}, no trailing comma")
                    # Continue scanning backwards for potential comma
                    k -= 1
                
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    if len(result) != len(text):
        print(f"DEBUG: Removed {len(text) - len(result)} characters")
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>str_replace</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>The file /workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py has been edited.    301	def loads(text, *args, **kwargs):
   302	    ''' Deserialize `text` (a `str` or `unicode` instance containing a JSON

    :param text: serialized JSON string with or without comments.
    :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.loads <http://docs.python.org/

    :returns: dict or list.

Let me add some debug to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/commentjson/commentjson.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):
    '''
    Remove trailing commas from arrays and objects in JSON text.
    This allows for more flexible JSON editing where trailing commas are permitted.
    '''
    result = text
    i = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                
                # Scan backwards, skipping whitespace and comments, looking for a comma
                while k > i:
                    # Determine if position k is inside a comment by scanning forward from i to k
                    pos = i
                    scan_in_string = False
                    scan_escape_next = False
                    scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                    scan_in_hash_comment = False
                    
                    while pos < k:
                        char = result[pos]
                        
                        # Handle escape sequences in strings
                        if scan_in_string and char == '\\' and not scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = True
                        elif scan_in_string and scan_escape_next:
                            scan_escape_next = False
                        # Handle string boundaries
                        elif not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                            scan_in_string = not scan_in_string
                        # Handle comments
                        elif not scan_in_string and not scan_in_single_line_comment and not scan_in_hash_comment:
                            if char == '/' and pos + 1 < len(result) and result[pos + 1] == '/':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = True
                            elif char == '#' and (pos == 0 or result[pos-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                                scan_in_hash_comment = True
                            elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                            elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                                scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        # Handle comment endings
                        elif scan_in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_single_line_comment = False
                        elif scan_in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                            scan_in_hash_comment = False
                        
                        pos += 1
                    
                    # At position k, check if we're in a comment
                    if scan_in_single_line_comment or scan_in_hash_comment or scan_in_string:
                        # Skip this character as it's inside a comment/string
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a whitespace character, skip it
                    if result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        k -= 1
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at the end of a // comment (the / character after another /)
                    if k > 0 and result[k] == '/' and result[k-1] == '/':
                        # Skip // comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 2
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we're at a # comment (the # character itself)
                    if result[k] == '#':
                        # Skip # comment backwards to start of line
                        k -= 1
                        while k > i and result[k] not in '\n\r':
                            k -= 1
                        if k > i:
                            k -= 1  # Skip newline
                        continue
                    
                    # If we found a comma, this is our trailing comma
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        break  # Done with this structure
                    
                    # If we found any other significant character, there's no trailing comma
                    # Continue scanning backwards for potential comma
                    k -= 1
                
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    return result</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>def remove_trailing_commas(text):

Let me create a more targeted test to understand what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/debug_step_by_step.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Step by step debug."""

import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8')

# Simple version to trace execution
def simple_remove_trailing_commas(text):
    print(f"Input length: {len(text)}")
    result = text
    i = 0
    structure_count = 0
    while i < len(result):
        if result[i] in '[{':
            structure_count += 1
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            print(f"Structure {structure_count}: Found {bracket_type} at position {i}")
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                if char == bracket_type:
                    level += 1
                elif char == closing_bracket:
                    level -= 1
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                print(f"  Matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                # Look for trailing comma
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                # Simple backwards scan (no comment handling for now)
                while k > i:
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        print(f"  Found trailing comma at position {k}")
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        j -= 1  # Adjust for removed character
                        break
                    elif result[k] not in ' \t\r\n':
                        print(f"  Found non-comma char '{result[k]}' at position {k}")
                        break
                    k -= 1
            else:
                print(f"  ERROR: Could not find matching closing bracket")
            
            i = j
            print(f"  Next i = {i}")
        else:
            i += 1
    
    print(f"Processed {structure_count} structures")
    print(f"Output length: {len(result)}")
    return result

def main():
    # Simple test case
    test1 = "[1, 2,]"
    print("=== Test 1 ===")

    result1 = simple_remove_trailing_commas(test1)
    print(f"Output: {repr(result1)}")
    
    print("\n=== Test 2 ===")
    test2 = '{"a": 1, "b": 2,}'
    print(f"Input:  {repr(test2)}")
    result2 = simple_remove_trailing_commas(test2)

Let me add some debug to my actual implementation to see what's happening:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8/debug_actual.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python
"""Debug actual implementation."""

import sys
sys.path.insert(0, '/workspace/vaidik__commentjson__0.8')

from commentjson.commentjson import remove_trailing_commas

# Add debug prints to a copy of the function
def debug_remove_trailing_commas(text):
    print(f"=== Processing text of length {len(text)} ===")
    result = text
    i = 0
    structure_count = 0
    while i < len(result):
        print(f"i={i}, char='{result[i] if i < len(result) else 'EOF'}'")
        if result[i] in '[{':
            structure_count += 1
            bracket_type = result[i]  # '[' or '{'
            closing_bracket = ']' if bracket_type == '[' else '}'
            print(f"Structure {structure_count}: Found {bracket_type} at position {i}")
            
            # Count nesting levels to find matching closing bracket
            level = 1
            j = i + 1
            in_string = False
            escape_next = False
            in_single_line_comment = False
            in_hash_comment = False
            
            print(f"  Scanning forward to find matching {closing_bracket}")
            while j < len(result) and level > 0:
                char = result[j]
                print(f"    j={j}, char='{char}', level={level}, in_string={in_string}")
                
                # Handle escape sequences in strings
                if in_string and char == '\\' and not escape_next:
                    escape_next = True
                elif in_string and escape_next:
                    escape_next = False
                # Handle string boundaries
                elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment and char == '"':
                    in_string = not in_string
                    print(f"    String boundary, in_string={in_string}")
                # Handle comments
                elif not in_string and not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                    if char == '/' and j + 1 < len(result) and result[j + 1] == '/':
                        in_single_line_comment = True
                        print(f"    Enter // comment")
                    elif char == '#' and (j == 0 or result[j-1] in ' \t\n\r'):
                        in_hash_comment = True
                        print(f"    Enter # comment")
                    elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_single_line_comment = False
                        print(f"    Exit // comment")
                    elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                        in_hash_comment = False
                        print(f"    Exit # comment")
                    # Handle nesting
                    elif not in_single_line_comment and not in_hash_comment:
                        if char == bracket_type:
                            level += 1
                            print(f"    Nest deeper, level={level}")
                        elif char == closing_bracket:
                            level -= 1
                            print(f"    Unnest, level={level}")
                elif in_single_line_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_single_line_comment = False
                    print(f"    Exit // comment (newline)")
                elif in_hash_comment and char in '\n\r':
                    in_hash_comment = False
                    print(f"    Exit # comment (newline)")
                
                j += 1
            
            if level == 0 and j <= len(result):  # Found matching closing bracket
                print(f"  Found matching {closing_bracket} at position {j-1}")
                # Now scan backwards from j-1 to i to find trailing commas
                k = j - 2  # Position just before closing bracket
                print(f"  Scanning backwards from {k} to {i}")
                
                # Scan backwards, skipping whitespace and comments, looking for a comma
                comma_found = False
                while k > i and not comma_found:
                    # Simplified backwards scan for debugging
                    if result[k] == ',':
                        print(f"  Found trailing comma at position {k}")
                        # Remove the comma
                        result = result[:k] + result[k+1:]
                        print(f"  Removed comma, new length {len(result)}")
                        # Adjust j since we removed a character
                        j -= 1
                        comma_found = True
                    elif result[k] in ' \t\r\n':
                        print(f"  Skipping whitespace at position {k}")
                        k -= 1
                    else:
                        print(f"  Found non-comma char '{result[k]}' at position {k}")
                        break  # No trailing comma
                
            else:
                print(f"  ERROR: Could not find matching closing bracket, level={level}")
            
            print(f"  Setting i = {j}")
            i = j
        else:
            i += 1
    
    print(f"=== Processed {structure_count} structures ===")
    return result

def main():
    test_nested = '{"arr": [1, 2,], "obj": {"x": 1,}}'
    print("Input:", repr(test_nested))
    result = debug_remove_trailing_commas(test_nested)
    print("Output:", repr(result))

if __name__ == "__main__":

The algorithm correctly identifies that there's only 1 top-level structure (the outer `{}`).     def loads(text, *args, **kwargs):
        ''' Deserialize `text` (a `str` or `unicode` instance containing a JSON
        document with Python or JavaScript like comments) to a Python object.

        :param text: serialized JSON string with or without comments.
        :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.loads <http://docs.python.org/
                       2/library/json.html#json.loads>`_ accepts.
        :returns: dict or list.
        '''

        if isinstance(text, (bytes, bytearray)):
            text = text.decode(detect_encoding(text), 'surrogatepass')

        # Remove trailing commas to support more flexible JSON editing
        text = remove_trailing_commas(text)

    def scan(i, to_scan):
        """The core Earley Scanner.

        This is a custom implementation of the scanner that uses the
        Lark lexer to match tokens. The scan list is built by the
        Earley predictor, based on the previously completed tokens.
        This ensures that at each phase of the parse we have a custom
        lexer context, allowing for more complex ambiguities."""

        node_cache = {}

        # 1) Loop the expectations and ask the lexer to match.
        # Since regexp is forward looking on the input stream, and we only
        # want to process tokens when we hit the point in the stream at which
        # they complete, we push all tokens into a buffer (delayed_matches), to
        # be held possibly for a later parse step when we reach the point in the
        # input stream at which they complete.
        for item in set(to_scan):
            m = match(item.expect, stream, i)
            if m:
                t = Token(item.expect.name, m.group(0), i, text_line, text_column)
                delayed_matches[m.end()].append( (item, i, t) )

                if self.complete_lex:
                    s = m.group(0)
                    for j in range(1, len(s)):
                        m = match(item.expect, s[:-j])
                        if m:
                            t = Token(item.expect.name, m.group(0), i, text_line, text_column)
                            delayed_matches[i+m.end()].append( (item, i, t) )

                # Remove any items that successfully matched in this pass from the to_scan buffer.
                # This ensures we don't carry over tokens that already matched, if we're ignoring below.
                to_scan.remove(item)

        # 3) Process any ignores. This is typically used for e.g. whitespace.
        # We carry over any unmatched items from the to_scan buffer to be matched again after
        # the ignore. This should allow us to use ignored symbols in non-terminals to implement
        # e.g. mandatory spacing.
        for x in self.ignore:
            m = match(x, stream, i)
            if m:
                # Carry over any items still in the scan buffer, to past the end of the ignored items.
                delayed_matches[m.end()].extend([(item, i, None) for item in to_scan ])

                # If we're ignoring up to the end of the file, # carry over the start symbol if it already completed.
                delayed_matches[m.end()].extend([(item, i, None) for item in columns[i] if item.is_complete and item.s == start_symbol])

        next_to_scan = set()
        next_set = set()
        columns.append(next_set)
        transitives.append({})

        ## 4) Process Tokens from delayed_matches.
        # This is the core of the Earley scanner. Create an SPPF node for each Token,
        # and create the symbol node in the SPPF tree. Advance the item that completed,
        # and add the resulting new item to either the Earley set (for processing by the
        # completer/predictor) or the to_scan buffer for the next parse step.
        for item, start, token in delayed_matches[i+1]:
            if token is not None:
                token.end_line = text_line
                token.end_column = text_column + 1

                new_item = item.advance()
                label = (new_item.s, new_item.start, i)
                new_item.node = node_cache[label] if label in node_cache else node_cache.setdefault(label, SymbolNode(*label))
                new_item.node.add_family(new_item.s, item.rule, new_item.start, item.node, token)
            else:
                new_item = item

            if new_item.expect in self.TERMINALS:
                # add (B ::= Aai+1.B, h, y) to Q'
                next_to_scan.add(new_item)
            else:
                # add (B ::= Aa+1.B, h, y) to Ei+1
                next_set.add(new_item)

        del delayed_matches[i+1]    # No longer needed, so unburden memory

        if not next_set and not delayed_matches and not next_to_scan:
>           raise UnexpectedCharacters(stream, i, text_line, text_column, {item.expect.name for item in to_scan}, set(to_scan))

    def loads(text, *args, **kwargs):
        ''' Deserialize `text` (a `str` or `unicode` instance containing a JSON
        document with Python or JavaScript like comments) to a Python object.

        :param text: serialized JSON string with or without comments.
        :param kwargs: all the arguments that `json.loads <http://docs.python.org/
                       2/library/json.html#json.loads>`_ accepts.
        :returns: dict or list.
        '''

        if isinstance(text, (bytes, bytearray)):
            text = text.decode(detect_encoding(text), 'surrogatepass')

        # Remove trailing commas to support more flexible JSON editing
        text = remove_trailing_commas(text)

        try:
            parsed = parser.parse(text)
            final_text = serializer.reconstruct(parsed)

Let me examine what went wrong. The issue is that my backwards scanning logic is flawed. When I scan backwards and find a comma, I assume it's a trailing comma, but it might actually be a legitimate separator.

A trailing comma is one that comes at the end of a list of items, followed only by whitespace and comments before the closing bracket. But a legitimate comma separates items within the list.

The key insight is that I need to be more careful about what constitutes a "trailing" comma. It's not just any comma found when scanning backwards - it's specifically a comma that has no subsequent items in the same structure.
